\section*{How to use this packet}
For each source claim, compare the exact source excerpts with the expanded
PaperInterface specification. Mark up this PDF or fill the blank reviewer box in the TeX source;
return the annotated file for reconciliation into the paper-local human-review
log.

\section*{Open the interactive dashboard}
The interactive dashboard is an optional alternative to using this PDF; it
presents the same review surface rather than an additional review requirement.
After forking and cloning (or pulling) AppliedModelingLib, run the following from the
repository root. The cache command is needed only the first time in a new clone.
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
cd <your-repository-checkout>
lake exe cache get
python3 scripts/review_dashboard.py --paper GHKR22BiasBounties --serve
\end{ReviewVerbatim}
Open \url{http://127.0.0.1:8765/} in a browser and keep that terminal running
while reviewing. The dashboard records saved annotations in this paper's local
reviewer-owned review record.

\paragraph{Lean declaration body}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
type:
{X : Type u_1} → {Y : Type u_2} → [inst : MeasurableSpace X] → MeasureTheory.Measure X → (X → Y → ℝ) → (X → Y) → Prop

value:
fun {X : Type u_1} {Y : Type u_2} [MeasurableSpace X] (law : MeasureTheory.Measure X) (risk : X → Y → ℝ)
    (current : X → Y) =>
  ∀ (group : Set X),
    MeasurableSet group →
      ∀ (alternative : X → Y),
        MeasureTheory.Integrable (fun (x : X) => risk x (alternative x)) law →
          ∫ (x : X) in group, risk x (current x) ∂law ≤ ∫ (x : X) in group, risk x (alternative x) ∂law
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

type:

value:
fun {X : Type u_1} [MeasurableSpace X] (law : MeasureTheory.Measure (X × Bool))
    (concepts : Set (AppliedModelingLib.Statistics.BinaryClassifier X)) (d count : ℕ) (delta : ℝ) =>
  MeasurableSet
      (AppliedModelingLib.Statistics.finiteIIDVCPopulationUnderestimateBadEvent law concepts count
        (AppliedModelingLib.Statistics.finiteIIDVCSourceConfidenceRadius d count delta)) ∧
    MeasurableSet
        (AppliedModelingLib.Statistics.finiteIIDVCPopulationOverestimateBadEvent law concepts count
          (AppliedModelingLib.Statistics.finiteIIDVCSourceConfidenceRadius d count delta)) ∧
      MeasurableSet
          (AppliedModelingLib.Statistics.finiteIIDVCTwoSampleBadEvent concepts count
            (AppliedModelingLib.Statistics.finiteIIDVCSourceConfidenceRadius d count delta -
              AppliedModelingLib.Statistics.finiteIIDVCSourceConfidenceRadius d count delta / 4)) ∧
        MeasurableSet
            (AppliedModelingLib.Statistics.finiteIIDVCPairSwapBadEvent concepts count
              (AppliedModelingLib.Statistics.finiteIIDVCSourceConfidenceRadius d count delta -
                AppliedModelingLib.Statistics.finiteIIDVCSourceConfidenceRadius d count delta / 4)) ∧
          MeasurableSet
            (AppliedModelingLib.Statistics.finiteIIDVCTwoSampleProductBadEvent concepts count
              (AppliedModelingLib.Statistics.finiteIIDVCSourceConfidenceRadius d count delta -
                AppliedModelingLib.Statistics.finiteIIDVCSourceConfidenceRadius d count delta / 4))
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}falsify\allowbreak{}Step\allowbreak{}From\allowbreak{}Decision}
\textbf{Declaration location:} paper-local
\paragraph{Lean declaration body}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
type:
{X : Type u_1} →
  {Y : Type u_2} →
    GHKR22BiasBounties.FalsifyState X Y →
      GHKR22BiasBounties.ProposedUpdate X Y →
        GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision → GHKR22BiasBounties.FalsifyState X Y

value:
fun {X : Type u_1} {Y : Type u_2} (state : GHKR22BiasBounties.FalsifyState X Y)
    (proposal : GHKR22BiasBounties.ProposedUpdate X Y) (decision : GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision) =>
  match decision with
  | GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision.rejected =>
    { current := state.current, accepted := state.accepted, reverseTranscript := decision :: state.reverseTranscript }
  | GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision.accepted =>
    { current := GHKR22BiasBounties.listUpdate state.current proposal.group proposal.replacement,
      accepted := state.accepted + 1, reverseTranscript := decision :: state.reverseTranscript }
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

type:

value:
fun {X : Type u_1} {Y : Type u_2} [MeasurableSpace X] [MeasurableSpace Y] (law : MeasureTheory.Measure (X × Y))
    (loss : GHKR22BiasBounties.BoundedLoss Y) (epsilon : ℝ) (current : GHKR22BiasBounties.Model X Y)
    (groups : List (GHKR22BiasBounties.Group X)) (pastModels : List (GHKR22BiasBounties.Model X Y)) =>
  ∀ group ∈ groups,
    ∀ past ∈ pastModels,
      0 < GHKR22BiasBounties.measureGroupMass law group →
        GHKR22BiasBounties.measureGroupLoss law loss current group ≤
          GHKR22BiasBounties.measureGroupLoss law loss past group +
            epsilon / GHKR22BiasBounties.measureGroupMass law group
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

type:

value:
fun {X : Type u_1} {Y : Type u_2} [MeasurableSpace X] [MeasurableSpace Y] (law : MeasureTheory.Measure (X × Y))
    (loss : GHKR22BiasBounties.BoundedLoss Y) (epsilon : ℝ) (submission : GHKR22BiasBounties.Submission X Y)
    (decision : GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision) =>
  (decision = GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision.rejected →
      ∀ (mu Delta : ℝ),
        epsilon ≤ mu * Delta →
          ¬GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureCertificateOfSuboptimality law loss submission.current submission.group
              submission.replacement mu Delta) ∧
    (decision = GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision.accepted →
      ∃ (mu : ℝ) (Delta : ℝ),
        GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureCertificateOfSuboptimality law loss submission.current submission.group
            submission.replacement mu Delta ∧
          epsilon / 2 ≤ mu * Delta)
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
type:
{X : Type u_1} →
  {Y : Type u_2} →
    [inst : MeasurableSpace X] →
      [inst_1 : MeasurableSpace Y] →
        MeasureTheory.Measure (X × Y) →
          GHKR22BiasBounties.BoundedLoss Y →
            ℝ →
              GHKR22BiasBounties.FalsifyState X Y →
                GHKR22BiasBounties.ProposedUpdate X Y → GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision → Prop

value:
fun {X : Type u_1} {Y : Type u_2} [MeasurableSpace X] [MeasurableSpace Y] (law : MeasureTheory.Measure (X × Y))
    (loss : GHKR22BiasBounties.BoundedLoss Y) (epsilon : ℝ) (state : GHKR22BiasBounties.FalsifyState X Y)
    (proposal : GHKR22BiasBounties.ProposedUpdate X Y) (decision : GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision) =>
  have next : GHKR22BiasBounties.FalsifyState X Y := GHKR22BiasBounties.falsifyStepFromDecision state proposal decision;
  (decision = GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision.rejected →
      ∀ (mu Delta : ℝ),
        epsilon ≤ mu * Delta →
          ¬GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureCertificateOfSuboptimality law loss state.current proposal.group
              proposal.replacement mu Delta) ∧
    (decision = GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision.accepted →
      ∃ (mu : ℝ) (Delta : ℝ),
        GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureCertificateOfSuboptimality law loss state.current proposal.group proposal.replacement
            mu Delta ∧
          epsilon / 2 ≤ mu * Delta ∧
            GHKR22BiasBounties.measureGroupLoss law loss next.current proposal.group =
                GHKR22BiasBounties.measureGroupLoss law loss proposal.replacement proposal.group ∧
              GHKR22BiasBounties.measureModelLoss law loss next.current ≤
                GHKR22BiasBounties.measureModelLoss law loss state.current - epsilon / 2)
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

value:
fun {X : Type u_1} {Y : Type u_2} (U : ℕ) (strategy : GHKR22BiasBounties.AdaptiveMonotoneProposalStrategy X Y)
    (state : GHKR22BiasBounties.MonotoneBountyState X Y) =>
  match state.remainingRepairs with
  | candidate :: tail => { current := state.current, group := candidate.group, replacement := candidate.replacement }
  | [] =>
    if state.externalProcessed < U then
      have proposal : GHKR22BiasBounties.ProposedUpdate X Y := strategy state.reverseTranscript.reverse;
      { current := state.current, group := proposal.group, replacement := proposal.replacement }
    else GHKR22BiasBounties.repairNoopSubmission state.current
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

fun {X : Type u_1} {Y : Type u_2} (state : GHKR22BiasBounties.MonotoneBountyState X Y) =>
  have candidates : List (GHKR22BiasBounties.ProposedUpdate X Y) :=
    GHKR22BiasBounties.repairCandidates state.protectedGroups state.publicModels;
  match candidates with
  | [] => GHKR22BiasBounties.publishMonotoneBountyState state
  | head :: tail =>
    { current := state.current, accepted := state.accepted, externalProcessed := state.externalProcessed,
      protectedGroups := state.protectedGroups, publicModels := state.publicModels, remainingRepairs := candidates,
      rejectedRepairsReverse := [], publicationsReverse := state.publicationsReverse,
      reverseTranscript := state.reverseTranscript }
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}monotone\allowbreak{}Bounty\allowbreak{}Step\allowbreak{}Core}
\textbf{Declaration location:} paper-local
\paragraph{Lean declaration body}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
type:
{X : Type u_1} →
  {Y : Type u_2} →
    ℕ →
      GHKR22BiasBounties.AdaptiveMonotoneProposalStrategy X Y →
        GHKR22BiasBounties.MonotoneBountyState X Y →
          GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision → GHKR22BiasBounties.MonotoneBountyState X Y

value:
fun {X : Type u_1} {Y : Type u_2} (U : ℕ) (strategy : GHKR22BiasBounties.AdaptiveMonotoneProposalStrategy X Y)
    (state : GHKR22BiasBounties.MonotoneBountyState X Y) (decision : GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision) =>
  match state.remainingRepairs with
  | candidate :: rest =>
    match decision with
    | GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision.rejected =>
      have advanced : GHKR22BiasBounties.MonotoneBountyState X Y :=
        { current := state.current, accepted := state.accepted, externalProcessed := state.externalProcessed,
          protectedGroups := state.protectedGroups, publicModels := state.publicModels, remainingRepairs := rest,
          rejectedRepairsReverse := candidate :: state.rejectedRepairsReverse,
          publicationsReverse := state.publicationsReverse, reverseTranscript := state.reverseTranscript };
      match rest with
      | [] => GHKR22BiasBounties.publishMonotoneBountyState advanced
      | head :: tail => advanced
    | GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision.accepted =>
      GHKR22BiasBounties.restartMonotoneRepairScan
        { current := GHKR22BiasBounties.listUpdate state.current candidate.group candidate.replacement,
          accepted := state.accepted + 1, externalProcessed := state.externalProcessed,
          protectedGroups := state.protectedGroups, publicModels := state.publicModels,
          remainingRepairs := state.remainingRepairs, rejectedRepairsReverse := state.rejectedRepairsReverse,
          publicationsReverse := state.publicationsReverse, reverseTranscript := state.reverseTranscript }
  | [] =>
    if state.externalProcessed < U then
      have proposal : GHKR22BiasBounties.ProposedUpdate X Y := strategy state.reverseTranscript.reverse;
      match decision with
      | GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision.rejected =>
        { current := state.current, accepted := state.accepted, externalProcessed := state.externalProcessed + 1,
          protectedGroups := state.protectedGroups, publicModels := state.publicModels,
          remainingRepairs := state.remainingRepairs, rejectedRepairsReverse := state.rejectedRepairsReverse,
          publicationsReverse := state.publicationsReverse, reverseTranscript := state.reverseTranscript }
      | GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision.accepted =>
        GHKR22BiasBounties.restartMonotoneRepairScan
          { current := GHKR22BiasBounties.listUpdate state.current proposal.group proposal.replacement,
            accepted := state.accepted + 1, externalProcessed := state.externalProcessed + 1,
            protectedGroups := proposal.group :: state.protectedGroups, publicModels := state.publicModels,
            remainingRepairs := state.remainingRepairs, rejectedRepairsReverse := state.rejectedRepairsReverse,
            publicationsReverse := state.publicationsReverse, reverseTranscript := state.reverseTranscript }
    else
      match decision with
      | GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision.rejected => state
      | GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision.accepted =>
        { current := state.current, accepted := state.accepted + 1, externalProcessed := state.externalProcessed,
          protectedGroups := state.protectedGroups, publicModels := state.publicModels,
          remainingRepairs := state.remainingRepairs, rejectedRepairsReverse := state.rejectedRepairsReverse,
          publicationsReverse := state.publicationsReverse, reverseTranscript := state.reverseTranscript }
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

fun {X : Type u_1} [MeasurableSpace X] (law : MeasureTheory.Measure (X × Bool))
    (current : GHKR22BiasBounties.Model X Bool) (G : Set (GHKR22BiasBounties.Group X))
    (H : Set (GHKR22BiasBounties.Model X Bool)) (tolerance : ℝ) {n : ℕ} (sample : Fin n → X × Bool) =>

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}Measure\allowbreak{}Monotone\allowbreak{}Bounty\allowbreak{}Conclusion}
\textbf{Declaration location:} paper-local
\paragraph{Lean declaration body}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
type:
{X : Type u_1} →
  {Y : Type u_2} →
    [inst : MeasurableSpace X] →
      [inst_1 : MeasurableSpace Y] →
        MeasureTheory.Measure (X × Y) →
          GHKR22BiasBounties.BoundedLoss Y →
            ℝ →
              {n : ℕ} →
                (Fin n → X × Y) →
                  ℕ → GHKR22BiasBounties.Model X Y → GHKR22BiasBounties.AdaptiveMonotoneProposalStrategy X Y → Prop

value:
fun {X : Type u_1} {Y : Type u_2} [MeasurableSpace X] [MeasurableSpace Y] (law : MeasureTheory.Measure (X × Y))
    (loss : GHKR22BiasBounties.BoundedLoss Y) (epsilon : ℝ) {n : ℕ} (sample : Fin n → X × Y) (U : ℕ)
    (initial : GHKR22BiasBounties.Model X Y) (strategy : GHKR22BiasBounties.AdaptiveMonotoneProposalStrategy X Y) =>
  have Q : ℕ := GHKR22BiasBounties.monotoneBountyQueryBudget epsilon U;
  have transcript : List GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision :=
    GHKR22BiasBounties.monotoneBountyTranscript epsilon loss sample U initial strategy;
  have output : GHKR22BiasBounties.MonotoneBountyState X Y :=
    GHKR22BiasBounties.monotoneBountyOutput epsilon loss sample U initial strategy;
  GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureMonotoneBountyRunGuaranteed law loss epsilon sample Q U initial strategy ∧
    transcript.length = Q ∧
      output.externalProcessed = U ∧
        output.remainingRepairs = [] ∧
          output.accepted ≤ GHKR22BiasBounties.checkerAcceptanceBudget epsilon ∧
            GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureMonotonePublicationsSound law loss epsilon output
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright Applied\allowbreak{}Modeling\allowbreak{}Lib.\allowbreak{}Learning.\allowbreak{}Prediction.\allowbreak{}Bounded\allowbreak{}Loss}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 310-\allowbreak{}311\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\section{Preliminaries}
We consider a supervised learning problem defined over \emph{labelled examples} $(x,y) \in \cX \times \cY$. This can represent (for example) a binary classification problem if $\cY = \{0,1\}$, a discrete multiclass classification problem if $\cY = \{1,\ldots,k\}$, or a regression problem if $\cY = \mathbb{R}$. We write $\cD$ to denote a joint probability distribution over labelled examples: $\cD \in \Delta (\cX \times \cY)$. We will write $D \sim \cD^n$ to denote a dataset consisting of $n$ labelled examples sampled i.i.d. from $\cD$. Our goal is to learn some \emph{model} represented as a function $f:\cX\rightarrow \cY$ which aims to predict the label of an example from its features, and we will evaluate the performance of our model with a loss function $\ell:\cY\times \cY \rightarrow [0,1]$, where $\ell(\hat y, y)$ represents the ``cost'' of mistakenly labelling an example that has true label $y$ with the prediction $f(x) = \hat y$. We will be interested in the performance of models $f$ not just \emph{overall} on the underlying distribution, but also on particular subgroups of interest. A subgroup corresponds to an arbitrary subset of the feature space $\cX$, which we will model using an indicator function:
\end{ReviewVerbatim}



\paragraph{Lean declaration type (metadata)}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
field value:
{Y : Type u_1} → AppliedModelingLib.Learning.Prediction.BoundedLoss Y → Y → Y → ℝ

field nonneg:
∀ {Y : Type u_1} (self : AppliedModelingLib.Learning.Prediction.BoundedLoss Y) (prediction truth : Y),
  0 ≤ self.value prediction truth

field le_one:
∀ {Y : Type u_1} (self : AppliedModelingLib.Learning.Prediction.BoundedLoss Y) (prediction truth : Y),
  self.value prediction truth ≤ 1
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\paragraph{Exact Lean library declaration}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
/-- A loss function normalized to the unit interval. -/
structure BoundedLoss (Y : Type*) where
  value : Y → Y → ℝ
  nonneg : ∀ prediction truth, 0 ≤ value prediction truth
  le_one : ∀ prediction truth, value prediction truth ≤ 1
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\paragraph{Recorded source-to-library screening}
\textbf{Verdict:} \texttt{matches}
\\
{\raggedright
\textbf{Reason:} The reusable structure is exactly the paper's primitive loss ell from two labels to a real value in [0,1]:\allowbreak{} the value field gives the loss, and the two proof fields enforce pointwise nonnegativity and the unit upper bound.\allowbreak{}
\par}
\reviewmatch{Matches source input}
\noindent\textbf{Reviewer annotation}\par

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}AE\allowbreak{}Bayes\allowbreak{}Optimal\allowbreak{}For\allowbreak{}Risk}
\noindent\textbf{Paper-local declaration:}\par
{\footnotesize\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}AE\allowbreak{}Bayes\allowbreak{}Optimal\allowbreak{}For\allowbreak{}Risk\par}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 333-\allowbreak{}339\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{definition}
A \emph{Bayes Optimal} model $f^*:\cX \rightarrow \cY$ with respect to a loss function $\ell$ and a distribution $\cD$ satisfies:
\ifdraft
$$f^*(x) \in \arg\min_{y \in \cY} \E_{(x', y') \sim \cD}\left[\ell(y, y') | x' = x\right]$$
\else $f^*(x) \in \arg\min_{y \in \cY} \E_{(x', y') \sim \cD}\left[\ell(y, y') | x' = x\right]$, \fi
where $f^*(x)$ can be defined arbitrarily for any $x$ that is not in the support of $\cD$.
\end{definition}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}


\paragraph{Formalized review target}
The archival source above is not asserted equivalent to this formalized target.
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
For an arbitrary population law, Bayes optimality means almost-everywhere minimization of conditional risk under a measurable regular conditional label kernel; off the governing feature law the model is irrelevant.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\textbf{Archival source anchor:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 333-\allowbreak{}339\par}
\paragraph{Lean-expanded paper semantic target}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
type:
{X : Type u_1} → {Y : Type u_2} → [inst : MeasurableSpace X] → MeasureTheory.Measure X → (X → Y → ℝ) → (X → Y) → Prop

value:
fun {X : Type u_1} {Y : Type u_2} [MeasurableSpace X] (law : MeasureTheory.Measure X) (risk : X → Y → ℝ)
    (current : X → Y) =>
  ∀ᵐ (x : X) ∂law, ∀ (prediction : Y), risk x (current x) ≤ risk x prediction
\end{ReviewVerbatim}


\paragraph{Recorded source-to-declaration screening}
\textbf{Verdict:} \texttt{matches\_approved\_corrected\_target}
\\
{\raggedright
\textbf{Reason:} Compared Definition 3 (main.\allowbreak{}tex:\allowbreak{}333-\allowbreak{}339) with the bound observation-\allowbreak{}4-\allowbreak{}pointwise-\allowbreak{}null-\allowbreak{}set correction.\allowbreak{} The actual predicate requires, on one feature-\allowbreak{}law almost-\allowbreak{}everywhere set, that current risk is no larger than the risk of every prediction.\allowbreak{} It imposes no restriction off that set and is the minimization predicate itself.\allowbreak{} The separately displayed kernelConditionalRisk supplies the conditional-\allowbreak{}loss integral for the source instantiation.\allowbreak{} This matches the expressly corrected almost-\allowbreak{}everywhere target, without claiming equivalence to the archival pointwise wording.\allowbreak{}
\par}
\reviewmatch{Matches formalized target}
\noindent\textbf{Reviewer annotation}\par
\reviewerbox
\clearpage

We consider a supervised learning problem defined over \emph{labelled examples} $(x,y) \in \cX \times \cY$. This can represent (for example) a binary classification problem if $\cY = \{0,1\}$, a discrete multiclass classification problem if $\cY = \{1,\ldots,k\}$, or a regression problem if $\cY = \mathbb{R}$. We write $\cD$ to denote a joint probability distribution over labelled examples: $\cD \in \Delta (\cX \times \cY)$. We will write $D \sim \cD^n$ to denote a dataset consisting of $n$ labelled examples sampled i.i.d. from $\cD$. Our goal is to learn some \emph{model} represented as a function $f:\cX\rightarrow \cY$ which aims to predict the label of an example from its features, and we will evaluate the performance of our model with a loss function $\ell:\cY\times \cY \rightarrow [0,1]$, where $\ell(\hat y, y)$ represents the ``cost'' of mistakenly labelling an example that has true label $y$ with the prediction $f(x) = \hat y$. We will be interested in the performance of models $f$ not just \emph{overall} on the underlying distribution, but also on particular subgroups of interest. A subgroup corresponds to an arbitrary subset of the feature space $\cX$, which we will model using an indicator function:
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}Measure\allowbreak{}Approx\allowbreak{}Bayes\allowbreak{}Optimal}
\noindent\textbf{Paper-local declaration:}\par
{\footnotesize\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}Measure\allowbreak{}Approx\allowbreak{}Bayes\allowbreak{}Optimal\par}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 354-\allowbreak{}361\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{definition}
A model $f:\cX\rightarrow \cY$ is $(\epsilon,\cC)$-Bayes optimal with respect to a collection of (group, model) pairs $\cC$ if for each $(g,h) \in \cC$, the performance of $f$ on $g$ is within $\epsilon$ of the performance of $h$ on $g$. In other words, for every $(g,h) \in \cC$:
\ifdraft
$$L(\cD,f,g) \leq L(\cD,h,g) + \frac{\epsilon}{\mu_g(\cD)}.$$
\else $L(\cD,f,g) \leq L(\cD,h,g) + \frac{\epsilon}{\mu_g(\cD)}.$ \fi
When $\cC$ is a product set $\cC = \cG\times \cH$, then we call $f$
``$(\epsilon,\cG,\cH)$-Bayes Optimal" and the condition is equivalent to requiring that for every $g \in G$, $f$ has performance on $g$ that is within $\epsilon$ of the \emph{best} model $h \in \cH$ on $g$. When $\cG$ and $\cH$ represent the set of all groups and models respectively, we call $f$ $\epsilon$-Bayes optimal.
\end{definition}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}group\allowbreak{}Indicator}
\noindent\textbf{Paper-local declaration:}\par
{\footnotesize\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}group\allowbreak{}Indicator\par}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 312-\allowbreak{}318\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{definition}[Subgroups]
A \emph{subgroup} of the feature space $\cX$ will be represented as an indicator function $g:\cX\rightarrow \{0,1\}$. We say that $x \in \cX$ is in group $g$ if $g(x) = 1$ and $x$ is \emph{not} in group $g$ otherwise. Given a group $g$, we write $\mu_g(\cD)$ to denote its measure under the probability distribution $\cD$:
\ifdraft
$$\mu_g(\cD) = \Pr_\cD[g(x) = 1].$$
\else $\mu_g(\cD) = \Pr_\cD[g(x) = 1].$ \fi
We write $\mu_g(D)$ to denote the corresponding empirical measure under $D$, which results from viewing $D$ as the uniform distribution over its elements.
\end{definition}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}measure\allowbreak{}Group\allowbreak{}Loss\allowbreak{}Numerator}
\noindent\textbf{Paper-local declaration:}\par

\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 321-\allowbreak{}330\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{definition}[Model Loss]
Given a model $f:\cX\rightarrow \cY$
We write $L(\cD,f)$ to denote the average loss of $f$ on distribution $\cD$:
\ifdraft
$$L(\cD,f) = \E_{(x,y) \sim \cD}[\ell(f(x),y)]$$
\else $L(\cD,f) = \E_{(x,y) \sim \cD}[\ell(f(x),y)]$. \fi
We  write $L(\cD,f,g)$ to denote the loss on $f$ conditional on membership in $g$:
$$L(\cD,f,g) = \E_{(x,y) \sim \cD}[\ell(f(x),y) | g(x) = 1].$$
Given a dataset $D$, we write $L(D,f)$ and $L(D,f,g)$ to denote the corresponding empirical losses on $D$, which result from viewing $D$ as the uniform distribution over its elements.
\end{definition}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}Measure\allowbreak{}Certificate\allowbreak{}Of\allowbreak{}Suboptimality}
\noindent\textbf{Paper-local declaration:}\par
{\footnotesize\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}Measure\allowbreak{}Certificate\allowbreak{}Of\allowbreak{}Suboptimality\par}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 377-\allowbreak{}384\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{definition}
A group indicator function $g:\cX\rightarrow \{0,1\}$ together with a model $h:\cX \rightarrow \cY$ form a $(\mu, \Delta)$-certificate of sub-optimality for a model $f$  under distribution $\cD$ if:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item Group $g$ has probability mass at least $\mu$ under $\cD$: $\mu_g(\cD) \geq \mu$, and
    \item $h$ improves on the performance of $f$ on group $g$ by at least $\Delta$: $L(\cD,f,g) \geq L(\cD,h,g) + \Delta$
\end{enumerate}
\ifdraft We say that $(g,h)$ form a certificate of sub-optimality for $f$ if they form a $(\mu,\Delta)$-certificate of optimality for $f$ for any constants $\mu,\Delta > 0$. \fi
\end{definition}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}Measure\allowbreak{}Cost\allowbreak{}Sensitive\allowbreak{}Minimizer}
\noindent\textbf{Paper-local declaration:}\par
{\footnotesize\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}Measure\allowbreak{}Cost\allowbreak{}Sensitive\allowbreak{}Minimizer\par}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 868-\allowbreak{}874\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{definition}
A cost-sensitive classification problem is defined by a model class $K$ consisting of functions $p:\cX\rightarrow \cY$, where $\cY$ is some finite label set,  a distribution $\cD$ over $\cX\times \cY$, and a set of real valued costs $c_{(x,y)}(\hat y)$ for each pair $(x, y)$ in the support of $\cD$ and each label $\hat y \in \cY$. A solution $p^* \in K$ to the cost-sensitive classification problem $(\cD, K, \{c_{(x,y)}\})$ is given by:
\ifdraft
$$p^* \in \arg\min_{p \in K} \E_{(x,y) \sim \cD}\left[ c_{(x,y)}(p(x))\right]$$
\else $p^* \in \arg\min_{p \in K} \E_{(x,y) \sim \cD}\left[ c_{(x,y)}(p(x))\right]$ \fi
i.e. the model that minimizes the expected costs for the labels it assigns to points drawn from $\cD$.
\end{definition}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}



\paragraph{Lean-expanded paper semantic target}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}

      GHKR22BiasBounties.Model X Bool →
        Set (GHKR22BiasBounties.TernaryPredictor X) → GHKR22BiasBounties.TernaryPredictor X → Prop

value:
fun {X : Type u_1} [MeasurableSpace X] (law : MeasureTheory.Measure (X × Bool))
    (current : GHKR22BiasBounties.Model X Bool) (K : Set (GHKR22BiasBounties.TernaryPredictor X))
    (pStar : GHKR22BiasBounties.TernaryPredictor X) =>
  pStar ∈ K ∧

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}adaptive\allowbreak{}Falsify\allowbreak{}And\allowbreak{}Update}
\noindent\textbf{Paper-local declaration:}\par
{\footnotesize\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}adaptive\allowbreak{}Falsify\allowbreak{}And\allowbreak{}Update\par}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 604-\allowbreak{}631\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{algorithm}
\ifdraft
\else
\small
\fi
\KwInput{An initial model $f_0$, a holdout dataset $D$, Target $\epsilon$, and a stream of submissions  $(g_1,h_1),(g_2,h_2),\ldots$ of length at most $U$}
Let $t \leftarrow 0$ \\

Initialize an instance of CertificateChecker($\epsilon, D, U, \ldots$)

\While{CertificateChecker has not halted}{
  Consider the next submission $(g_i,h_i)$ \\

  Feed the triple $(f_t,g_i,h_i)$ to CertificateChecker and receive $\pi_i\in \{\bot,\top\}$ \\

  \If{$\pi_i = \bot$}{
    \KwOut{Submission $(g_i,h_i)$ is rejected.}
  }
  \Else{
   Let $t \leftarrow t+1$ and let $f_t = \textrm{ListUpdate}(f_{t-1},(g_i,h_i))$ \\

   \KwOut{Submission $(g_i,h_i)$ is accepted. The new model is $f_{t}$.}
  }

}
\caption{FalsifyAndUpdate($\epsilon,D,(f_1,g_1,h_1),\ldots )$: An algorithm that maintains a sequence of models $f_1,\ldots,f_T$ and accepts submissions of proposed certificates of sub-optimality $(g_i,h_i)$ that attempt to falsify the assertion that the current model $f_t$ is not $\epsilon$-Bayes optimal. It either accepts or rejects each submission: if it accepts a submission then it also updates its current model $f_t$ and outputs a new model $f_{t+1}$.}
\label{alg:FalsifyAndUpdate}
\end{algorithm}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}certificate\allowbreak{}Checker\allowbreak{}Decision}
\noindent\textbf{Paper-local declaration:}\par
{\footnotesize\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}certificate\allowbreak{}Checker\allowbreak{}Decision\par}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 524-\allowbreak{}547\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{algorithm}
\ifdraft
\else
\small
\fi
\KwInput{Holdout dataset $D$, Target $\epsilon$, and a stream of submissions  $(f_1,g_1,h_1),(f_2,g_2,h_2),\ldots$ of length at most $U$}
$\mathbf{NumberAccepted} \leftarrow 0$

\While{$\mathbf{NumberAccepted} \leq 2/\epsilon$ and $i \leq U$}{
  Consider the next submission $(f_i,g_i,h_i)$ \\

  Compute $\mu_i \leftarrow \mu_D(g_i)$, $\Delta_i \leftarrow L(D,f_i,g_i) - L(D,h_i,g_i)$

  \If{$\mu_i\cdot \Delta_i < \frac{3\epsilon}{4}$} {
    \KwOut{$\pi_i = \bot$ (Submission Rejected)}
  }
  \Else{
    \KwOut{$\pi_i = \top$ (Submission Accepted)}
    $\mathbf{NumberAccepted} \leftarrow \mathbf{NumberAccepted}$+1.
  }
}
\caption{CertificateChecker($\epsilon,D,U,(f_1,g_1,h_1),\ldots )$: An algorithm that takes as input a stream of submissions $(f_i,g_i,h_i)$ and checks if $(g_i,h_i)$ is a certificate of sub-optimality for $f_i$ with sufficiently high parameter values.}
\label{alg:checker}
\end{algorithm}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}



\paragraph{Lean-expanded paper semantic target}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
type:
{X : Type u_1} →
  {Y : Type u_2} →
    {n : ℕ} →
      ℝ →
        GHKR22BiasBounties.BoundedLoss Y →
          (Fin n → X × Y) → GHKR22BiasBounties.Submission X Y → GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision

value:
fun {X : Type u_1} {Y : Type u_2} {n : ℕ} (epsilon : ℝ) (loss : GHKR22BiasBounties.BoundedLoss Y)
    (sample : Fin n → X × Y) (submission : GHKR22BiasBounties.Submission X Y) =>
  if GHKR22BiasBounties.empiricalSubmissionScore loss sample submission < 3 * epsilon / 4 then
    GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision.rejected
  else GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision.accepted
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}derived\allowbreak{}Group}
\noindent\textbf{Paper-local declaration:}\par
{\footnotesize\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}derived\allowbreak{}Group\par}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 847-\allowbreak{}865\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{definition}
Given a ternary valued function $p:\cX\rightarrow \{0,1,?\}$, the $p$-derived group $g_p$ and model $h_p$ are defined as:
\ifdraft
$$g_p(x) = \begin{cases}
      1 & \text{if } p(x) \in \{0,1\} \\
      0 & \text{if } p(x) = ?
   \end{cases} \ \ \ \ \ \ \  \ h_p(x) = \begin{cases}
      p(x) & \text{if } p(x) \in \{0,1\} \\
      0 & \text{if } p(x) = ?
   \end{cases}$$
\else $g_p(x) = \begin{cases}
      1 & \text{if } p(x) \in \{0,1\} \\
      0 & \text{if } p(x) = ?
   \end{cases} \ \ \ \ \ \ \  \ h_p(x) = \begin{cases}
      p(x) & \text{if } p(x) \in \{0,1\} \\
      0 & \text{if } p(x) = ?
   \end{cases}$ \fi
In other words, interpreting $p(x) = ?$ as the decision for $p$ to ``defer'' on $x$, $g_p$ defines exactly the group of examples that $p$ does \emph{not} defer on, and $h_p$ is the model that makes the same prediction as $p$ on every example that $p$ does not defer on. Given a class of ternary functions $K$, let the $K$-derived certificates $\cC_K$ denote the set of pairs $(g_p,h_p)$ that can be so derived from some $p \in K$: $\cC_K = \{(g_p,h_p) : p \in K\}$. Similarly let $\cG_K = \{g_p : p \in K\}$ and $\cH_K = \{h_p : p \in K\}$ denote the class of group indicator functions and models derived from $K$ respectively.
\end{definition}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\begin{ReviewVerbatim}

\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\end{itemize}

\begin{algorithm}[H]
\ifdraft
\else
\small
\fi
\KwInput{A model $f_t$, and a certificate of suboptimality $(g_{t+1},h_{t+1})$}
Define $f_{t+1}$ as follows:
\[ f_{t+1}(x)= \begin{cases}
      f_t(x) & \text{if } g_{t+1}(x)=0 \\
      h_{t+1}(x) & \text{if } g_{t+1}(x)=1
   \end{cases}
\]

\KwOutput{$f_{t+1}$}
\caption{ListUpdate($f_t,(g_{t+1},h_{t+1})$): An Update Algorithm Constructing a Decision List}
\label{alg:basic}
\end{algorithm}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}falsify\allowbreak{}And\allowbreak{}Update\allowbreak{}Step}
\noindent\textbf{Paper-local declaration:}\par
{\footnotesize\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}falsify\allowbreak{}And\allowbreak{}Update\allowbreak{}Step\par}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 604-\allowbreak{}631\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{algorithm}
\ifdraft
\else
\small
\fi
\KwInput{An initial model $f_0$, a holdout dataset $D$, Target $\epsilon$, and a stream of submissions  $(g_1,h_1),(g_2,h_2),\ldots$ of length at most $U$}
Let $t \leftarrow 0$ \\

Initialize an instance of CertificateChecker($\epsilon, D, U, \ldots$)

\While{CertificateChecker has not halted}{
  Consider the next submission $(g_i,h_i)$ \\

  Feed the triple $(f_t,g_i,h_i)$ to CertificateChecker and receive $\pi_i\in \{\bot,\top\}$ \\

  \If{$\pi_i = \bot$}{
    \KwOut{Submission $(g_i,h_i)$ is rejected.}
  }
  \Else{
   Let $t \leftarrow t+1$ and let $f_t = \textrm{ListUpdate}(f_{t-1},(g_i,h_i))$ \\

   \KwOut{Submission $(g_i,h_i)$ is accepted. The new model is $f_{t}$.}
  }

}
\caption{FalsifyAndUpdate($\epsilon,D,(f_1,g_1,h_1),\ldots )$: An algorithm that maintains a sequence of models $f_1,\ldots,f_T$ and accepts submissions of proposed certificates of sub-optimality $(g_i,h_i)$ that attempt to falsify the assertion that the current model $f_t$ is not $\epsilon$-Bayes optimal. It either accepts or rejects each submission: if it accepts a submission then it also updates its current model $f_t$ and outputs a new model $f_{t+1}$.}
\label{alg:FalsifyAndUpdate}
\end{algorithm}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}



\paragraph{Lean-expanded paper semantic target}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
type:
{X : Type u_1} →
  {Y : Type u_2} →
    {n : ℕ} →
      ℝ →
        GHKR22BiasBounties.BoundedLoss Y →
          (Fin n → X × Y) →
            GHKR22BiasBounties.FalsifyState X Y →
              GHKR22BiasBounties.ProposedUpdate X Y → GHKR22BiasBounties.FalsifyState X Y

value:
fun {X : Type u_1} {Y : Type u_2} {n : ℕ} (epsilon : ℝ) (loss : GHKR22BiasBounties.BoundedLoss Y)
    (sample : Fin n → X × Y) (state : GHKR22BiasBounties.FalsifyState X Y)
    (proposal : GHKR22BiasBounties.ProposedUpdate X Y) =>
  have decision : GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision :=
    GHKR22BiasBounties.certificateCheckerDecision epsilon loss sample
      (GHKR22BiasBounties.proposalSubmission state proposal);
  match decision with
  | GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision.rejected =>
    { current := state.current, accepted := state.accepted, reverseTranscript := decision :: state.reverseTranscript }
  | GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision.accepted =>
    { current := GHKR22BiasBounties.listUpdate state.current proposal.group proposal.replacement,
      accepted := state.accepted + 1, reverseTranscript := decision :: state.reverseTranscript }
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}induced\allowbreak{}Cost}
\noindent\textbf{Paper-local declaration:}\par
{\footnotesize\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}induced\allowbreak{}Cost\par}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 878-\allowbreak{}889\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{definition}
Given a binary model $f:\cX\rightarrow \cY$, the induced costs of $f$  are defined as follows:
\ifdraft
$$c^f_{(x,y)}(\hat y) = \begin{cases}
      0 & \text{if } \hat y = ? \\
      1 & \text{if } f(x) = y \neq \hat y \\
      -1 & \text{if } \hat y = y \neq f(x) \\
      0 & \text{otherwise.}
   \end{cases}$$
\else $c^f_{(x,y)}(\hat y) = 0$ if $\hat y = ?$ or if $\hat y = f(x)$, $c^f_{(x,y)}(\hat y) = 1$ if $f(x) = y \neq \hat y$, and $c^f_{(x,y)}(\hat y) = -1$ if $\hat y = y \neq f(x)$. \fi
Intuitively, it costs nothing to defer a decision to the existing model $f$ --- or equivalently, to make the same decision as $f$. On the other hand, making the \emph{wrong} decision on an example $x$ costs $1$ when $f$ would have made the right decision, and making the right decision ``earns'' $1$ when $f$ would not have.
\end{definition}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}



\paragraph{Lean-expanded paper semantic target}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
type:
{X : Type u_1} → GHKR22BiasBounties.Model X Bool → X × Bool → GHKR22BiasBounties.TernaryLabel → ℝ

value:
fun {X : Type u_1} (current : GHKR22BiasBounties.Model X Bool) (datum : X × Bool)
    (prediction : GHKR22BiasBounties.TernaryLabel) =>
  match prediction with
  | GHKR22BiasBounties.TernaryLabel.defer => 0
  | label =>
    GHKR22BiasBounties.binaryZeroOneLoss.value (GHKR22BiasBounties.ternaryBinaryPrediction label) datum.2 -
      GHKR22BiasBounties.binaryZeroOneLoss.value (current datum.1) datum.2
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{definition}
A \emph{Bayes Optimal} model $f^*:\cX \rightarrow \cY$ with respect to a loss function $\ell$ and a distribution $\cD$ satisfies:
\ifdraft
$$f^*(x) \in \arg\min_{y \in \cY} \E_{(x', y') \sim \cD}\left[\ell(y, y') | x' = x\right]$$
\else $f^*(x) \in \arg\min_{y \in \cY} \E_{(x', y') \sim \cD}\left[\ell(y, y') | x' = x\right]$, \fi
where $f^*(x)$ can be defined arbitrarily for any $x$ that is not in the support of $\cD$.
\end{definition}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}measure\allowbreak{}Coordinate\allowbreak{}Ascent\allowbreak{}Step}
\noindent\textbf{Paper-local declaration:}\par
{\footnotesize\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}measure\allowbreak{}Coordinate\allowbreak{}Ascent\allowbreak{}Step\par}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 991-\allowbreak{}1005\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{algorithm}
\KwInput{A dataset $D$, a model $f_{t-1}$, group and model classes $\cG$ and $\cH$, and an error parameter $\epsilon$.}
Let $(g^*,h^*) \in \cG \times \cH$ be an arbitrary initial certificate.  \\
CurrentValue $\leftarrow \mu_{D}(g^*)\cdot (L(D,f_{t-1},g^*) - L(D,h^*,g^*))$ \\
Let:
$$h^* = \arg\min_{h \in \cH} L(D_{g^*},h) \ \ \ g^* = \arg\min_{g \in \cG} L(D_{h^*},g)$$
\While{$\mu_{D}(g^*)\cdot (L(D,f_{t-1},g^*) - L(D,h^*,g^*) \geq \textrm{CurrentValue}+\epsilon$}{
CurrentValue $\leftarrow \mu_{D}(g^*)\cdot (L(D,f_{t-1},g^*) - L(D,h^*,g^*))$ \\
Let:
$$h^* = \arg\min_{h \in \cH} L(D_{g^*},h) \ \ \ g^* = \arg\min_{g \in \cG} L(D_{h^*},g)$$
}
\KwOut{Certificate $(g_t,h_t) = (g^*,h^*)$}
\caption{AltMinCertificateFinder($D,f_{t-1},\cG,\cH,\epsilon)$: An algorithm for finding $\epsilon$-locally optimal certificates of sub-optimality $(g_t,h_t) \in \cG\times \cH$ for model $f_{t-1}$.}
\label{alg:altmin}
\end{algorithm}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}


\paragraph{Formalized review target}
The archival source above is not asserted equivalent to this formalized target.
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
At each state, test the exact model best-response gap and the exact group best-response gap separately, take one coordinate move only if its gap exceeds epsilon, and halt only when neither gap does.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\textbf{Archival source anchor:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 991-\allowbreak{}1005\par}
\paragraph{Lean-expanded paper semantic target}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
type:
{X : Type u_1} →
  [inst : MeasurableSpace X] →
    (law : MeasureTheory.Measure (X × Bool)) →
      (current : GHKR22BiasBounties.Model X Bool) →
        ℝ →
          {G : Set (GHKR22BiasBounties.Group X)} →
            {H : Set (GHKR22BiasBounties.Model X Bool)} →
              GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureCoordinateBestResponses law current G H →
                GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificatePair X → Option (GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificatePair X)

value:
fun {X : Type u_1} [MeasurableSpace X] (law : MeasureTheory.Measure (X × Bool))
    (current : GHKR22BiasBounties.Model X Bool) (epsilon : ℝ) {G : Set (GHKR22BiasBounties.Group X)}
    {H : Set (GHKR22BiasBounties.Model X Bool)}
    (oracle : GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureCoordinateBestResponses law current G H)
    (pair : GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificatePair X) =>
  have modelResponse : GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificatePair X :=
    { group := pair.group, replacement := oracle.bestModel pair.group };
  if
      GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureBinaryCertificateObjective law current pair.group pair.replacement + epsilon <
        GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureBinaryCertificateObjective law current modelResponse.group
          modelResponse.replacement then
    some modelResponse
  else
    have groupResponse : GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificatePair X :=
      { group := oracle.bestGroup pair.replacement, replacement := pair.replacement };
    if
        GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureBinaryCertificateObjective law current pair.group pair.replacement + epsilon <
          GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureBinaryCertificateObjective law current groupResponse.group
            groupResponse.replacement then
      some groupResponse
    else none
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}measure\allowbreak{}Model\allowbreak{}Loss}
\noindent\textbf{Paper-local declaration:}\par
{\footnotesize\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}measure\allowbreak{}Model\allowbreak{}Loss\par}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 321-\allowbreak{}330\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{definition}[Model Loss]
Given a model $f:\cX\rightarrow \cY$
We write $L(\cD,f)$ to denote the average loss of $f$ on distribution $\cD$:
\ifdraft
$$L(\cD,f) = \E_{(x,y) \sim \cD}[\ell(f(x),y)]$$
\else $L(\cD,f) = \E_{(x,y) \sim \cD}[\ell(f(x),y)]$. \fi
We  write $L(\cD,f,g)$ to denote the loss on $f$ conditional on membership in $g$:
$$L(\cD,f,g) = \E_{(x,y) \sim \cD}[\ell(f(x),y) | g(x) = 1].$$
Given a dataset $D$, we write $L(D,f)$ and $L(D,f,g)$ to denote the corresponding empirical losses on $D$, which result from viewing $D$ as the uniform distribution over its elements.
\end{definition}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}monotone\allowbreak{}Bounty\allowbreak{}Output}
\noindent\textbf{Paper-local declaration:}\par
{\footnotesize\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}monotone\allowbreak{}Bounty\allowbreak{}Output\par}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 671-\allowbreak{}713\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{algorithm}
\ifdraft\else
\small
\fi
\KwInput{A model $f_0$, a holdout dataset $D$, Target $\epsilon$, and a stream of submissions  $(g_1,h_1),(g_2,h_2),\ldots$ of length at most $U$}
Let $t \leftarrow 0$ and Initialize an instance of CertificateChecker($\epsilon, D, U + \frac{8}{\epsilon^3}, \ldots$)

\While{CertificateChecker has not halted}{
  Consider the next submission $(g_i,h_i)$ \\

  Feed the triple $(f_t,g_i,h_i)$ to CertificateChecker and receive $\pi_i\in \{\bot,\top\}$ \\

  \If{$\pi_i = \bot$}{
    \KwOut{Submission $(g_i,h_i)$ is rejected.}
  }
  \Else{
    MonotoneProgress $\leftarrow$ FALSE \\
    $(g_U,h_U) \leftarrow (g_i,h_i)$ \\
    $t' \leftarrow t$ and $f'_{t'} \leftarrow f_t$ \\
  \While{MonotoneProgress $=$ FALSE and CertificateChecker has not halted}{
    Let $t' \leftarrow t'+1$ and let $f'_{t'} = \textrm{ListUpdate}(f_{t'-1}',(g_U,h_U))$ \\
    MonotoneProgress $\leftarrow$ TRUE

    \For{each pair $\ell < t$, $g_j \in
    \cG(f_t)$}{
      Feed the triple $(f_t,g_j,f_{\ell})$ to CertificateChecker and receive $\pi_U \in \{\bot,\top\}$ \\
      \If{$\pi_U = \top$ (Submission accepted)} {
        MonotoneProgress $\leftarrow$ FALSE \\
        $(g_U,h_U) \leftarrow (g_j, f_\ell)$
      }

    }

   }
   Let $t \leftarrow t+1$ and let $f_t = f'_{t'}$ \\

   \KwOut{Submission $(g_i,h_i)$ is accepted. The new model is $f_{t}$.}
  }

}
\caption{MonotoneFalsifyAndUpdate($\epsilon,D,(f_1,g_1,h_1),\ldots )$: A version of FalsifyAndUpdate that guarantees approximate group-wise error monotonicity.}
\label{alg:MonFalsifyAndUpdate}
\end{algorithm}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}train\allowbreak{}By\allowbreak{}Opt\allowbreak{}Run}
\noindent\textbf{Paper-local declaration:}\par
{\footnotesize\ttfamily\raggedright GHKR22\allowbreak{}Bias\allowbreak{}Bounties.\allowbreak{}train\allowbreak{}By\allowbreak{}Opt\allowbreak{}Run\par}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 786-\allowbreak{}811\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{algorithm}
\ifdraft
\else
\small
\fi
\KwInput{A dataset $D$, a set of group/model pairs $\cC$, and accuracy parameter $\epsilon$.}
Let $f_0:\cX\rightarrow \{0,1\}$ be an arbitrary initial model and $T = 2/\epsilon$ \\

Randomly divide $D$ into $T$ equally sized datasets: $D_1,\ldots,D_T$.

\For{$t = 1$ to $T$}{
Let
$$(g_t,h_t) =\arg\max_{(g,h) \in \cC }\mu_{D}(g)\cdot (L(D,f_{t-1},g) - L(D,h,g))$$

\If{$\mu_{D}(g_t)\cdot (L(D,f_{t-1},g_t) - L(D,h_t,g_t)) \leq \frac{3\epsilon}{4}$}{
\KwOut{Model $f_{t-1}$}
}
\Else{
Let $f_t = \textrm{ListUpdate}(f_{t-1},(g_t,h_t))$
}
}
\KwOut{Model $f_T$}

\caption{TrainByOpt($D,\cC,\epsilon)$: An algorithm for training an $(\epsilon,\cC)$-Bayes optimal model given the ability to optimize over certificates $(g,h) \in \cC$.}
\label{alg:TrainByOpt}
\end{algorithm}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}


\paragraph{Formalized review target}
The archival source above is not asserted equivalent to this formalized target.
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
TrainByOpt optimizes and tests on the fresh block D_t at round t and runs for ceil(2/epsilon) rounds, applying ListUpdate exactly when the empirical objective exceeds 3 epsilon / 4.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\paragraph{Verbatim source input}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{observation}
\label{obs:BO}
Fixing a loss function $\ell$ and a distribution $\cD$, $f^*$ is a Bayes optimal model if and only if for every group $g$ and every alternative model $h$:
\ifdraft
$$L(\cD, f^*, g) \leq L(\cD, h, g)$$
\else $L(\cD, f^*, g) \leq L(\cD, h, g)$.\fi
\end{observation}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}


\paragraph{Formalized review target}
The archival source above is not asserted equivalent to this formalized target.
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
Under a measurable conditional-label kernel and an arbitrary label space, a model is almost everywhere no worse than every integrable alternative model if and only if no such alternative improves its integrated conditional risk on any measurable subgroup; for countable labels this agrees with common-null-set actionwise Bayes optimality.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\section*{Review row 3}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 426-\allowbreak{}433\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim source input}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{restatable}{theorem}{progress}
\label{thm:progress}
Algorithm \ref{alg:basic} (ListUpdate) has the following properties. If $(g_{t+1},h_{t+1})$ form a $(\mu,\Delta)$-certificate of sub-optimality for $f_{t}$, and $f_{t+1} = \textrm{ListUpdate}(f_t,(g_{t+1},h_{t+1}))$ then:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item The new model matches the performance of $h_{t+1}$ on group $g_{t+1}$: $L(\cD,f_{t+1},g_{t+1}) = L(\cD, h_{t+1},g_{t+1})$, and
    \item The overall performance of the model is improved by at least $\mu\cdot \Delta$: $L(\cD, f_{t+1}) \leq L(\cD, f_t) - \mu\cdot \Delta$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{restatable}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\section*{Review row 4}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 461-\allowbreak{}464\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim source input}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{restatable}{theorem}{updatebound}
\label{thm:updatebound}
Fix any $\epsilon > 0$. For any initial model $f_0$ with loss $L(\cD,f_0) = \ell_0$ and any sequence of models $f_1,\ldots,f_T$, such that $f_i = \textrm{ListUpdate}(f_{i-1},(g_{i},h_{i}))$ and each pair $(g_i,h_{i})$ forms a $(\mu,\Delta)$-certificate of suboptimality for $f_{i-1}$ for some $\mu, \Delta$ such that  $\mu\cdot \Delta \geq \epsilon$, the length of the update sequence must be at most $T \leq \frac{\ell_0}{\epsilon} \leq \frac{1}{\epsilon}$.
\end{restatable}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

    ∀ (epsilon : ℝ),
      0 < epsilon →
        ∀ (initial : GHKR22BiasBounties.Model X Y) (updates : List (GHKR22BiasBounties.Update X Y)),
          GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureValidUpdateSequence law loss epsilon initial updates →
            ↑updates.length ≤ GHKR22BiasBounties.measureModelLoss law loss initial / epsilon ∧
              GHKR22BiasBounties.measureModelLoss law loss initial / epsilon ≤ 1 / epsilon
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\section*{Review row 5}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 549-\allowbreak{}561\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim source input}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{restatable}{theorem}{certchecker}
\label{thm:adaptive}
Let $\cD \in \Delta(\cX \times \cY)$ be any distribution over labelled examples, and let $D \sim \cD^n$ be a holdout dataset consisting of $n$ i.i.d. samples from $\cD$. Suppose:
\ifdraft
$$n \geq \frac{65 \ln(2U/\delta')}{\epsilon^3}$$
\else $n \geq \frac{65 \ln(2U/\delta')}{\epsilon^3}$. \fi
Let $\pi$ be the output stream generated by CertificateChecker$(\epsilon,D,(f_1,g_1,h_1),\ldots)$ (Algorithm \ref{alg:checker}).
Then for any possibly adaptive process generating a stream of up to $U$ submissions $(f_i,g_i,h_i)$ as a function of the output stream $\pi \in \{\bot,\top\}^*$, with probability $1-\delta$ over the randomness of $D$:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item For every round $i$ such that $\pi_i = \bot$ (the submission is rejected), we have that $(g_i,h_i)$ is not a $(\mu,\Delta)$ certificate of sub-optimality for $f_i$ for any $(\mu,\Delta)$ with $\mu\cdot \Delta \geq \epsilon$. And:
    \item For every round $i$ such that $\pi_i = \top$ (the submission is accepted), we have that $(g_i,h_i)$ is a $(\mu,\Delta)$-certificate of sub-optimality for $f_i$ for $\mu\cdot\Delta \geq \epsilon/2$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{restatable}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}


\paragraph{Formalized review target}
The archival source above is not asserted equivalent to this formalized target.
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
For every adaptive strategy, the probability that any issued checker decision violates the epsilon-rejection or epsilon/2-acceptance semantics is at most 2 U (U+1)^floor(2/epsilon) exp(-n epsilon^2/32).
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\textbf{Archival source anchor:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 549-\allowbreak{}561\par}

    ∀ (epsilon : ℝ),
      0 < epsilon →
        ∀ (n U : ℕ),
          0 < n →
            ∀ (strategy : GHKR22BiasBounties.AdaptiveSubmissionStrategy X Y),
              (∀ (transcript : List GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificateDecision),
                  GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureSubmissionMeasurable (strategy transcript)) →
                GHKR22BiasBounties.measureCertificateCheckerRunGuaranteeFailure law loss epsilon n U strategy ≤

\section*{Review row 6}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 633-\allowbreak{}645\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim source input}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{restatable}{theorem}{bountySC}
\label{thm:bounty-SC}
Fix any $\epsilon,\delta > 0$. Let $\cD \in \Delta(\cX \times \cY)$ be any distribution over labelled examples, and let $D \sim \cD^n$ be a holdout dataset consisting of $n$ i.i.d. samples from $\cD$. Suppose:
\ifdraft
$$n \geq \frac{65 \ln(2U/\delta')}{\epsilon^3}.$$
\else $n \geq \frac{65 \ln(2U/\delta')}{\epsilon^3}.$ \fi
Then for any (possibly adaptive) process generating a sequence of at most $U$ submissions $\{(g_i,h_i)\}_{i=1}^U$, with probability at least $1-\delta$, we have that FalsifyAndUpdate$(\epsilon,D,\cdots)$ satisfies:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item If $(g_i,h_i)$ is rejected, then $(g_i,h_i)$ is not a $(\mu,\Delta)$-certificate of sub-optimality for $f_t$, where $f_t$ is the current model at the time of submission $i$, for any $\mu, \Delta$ such that $\mu\cdot \Delta \geq \epsilon$.
    \item If $(g_i,h_i)$ is accepted, then $(g_i,h_i)$ is a $(\mu,\Delta)$-certificate of sub-optimality for $f_t$, where $f_t$ is the current model at the time of submission $i$, for some $\mu, \Delta$ such that $\mu\cdot \Delta \geq \frac{\epsilon}{2}$. Moreover, the new model $f_{t+1}$ output satisfies $L(\cD,f_{t+1}, g_i) = L(\cD, h_i, g_i)$ and $L(\cD,f_{t+1}) \leq L(\cD,f_t) - \frac{\epsilon}{2}$.
    \item FalsifyAndUpdate does not halt before receiving all $U$ submissions.
\end{enumerate}
\end{restatable}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}


\paragraph{Formalized review target}
The archival source above is not asserted equivalent to this formalized target.
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
The complete adaptive FalsifyAndUpdate failure event, including incorrect decisions, premature termination, and an excess update count, has the same explicit exact sparse-transcript tail as the checker.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\section*{Review row 7}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 659-\allowbreak{}661\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim source input}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{remark}
Note that FalsifyAndUpdate has sample complexity scaling only logarithmically with the total number of submissions $U$ that we can accept, and no dependence on the complexity of the submissions. This means that a relatively small holdout dataset $D$ is sufficient to run an extremely long-running bias bounty program (i.e. handling a number of submissions that is exponentially large in the size of the holdout set) that automatically updates the current model whenever submissions are accepted and bounties are awarded.
\end{remark}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}



\paragraph{Expanded PaperInterface specification}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
∀ {X : Type u_1} {Y : Type u_2} [inst : MeasurableSpace X] [inst_1 : MeasurableSpace Y]
  (law : MeasureTheory.Measure (X × Y)) [MeasureTheory.IsProbabilityMeasure law]
  {loss : GHKR22BiasBounties.BoundedLoss Y},
  GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasurableBoundedLoss loss →
    ∀ (U K : ℕ) (family : GHKR22BiasBounties.AdaptiveQueryIndex U K → GHKR22BiasBounties.Submission X Y),
      (∀ (query : GHKR22BiasBounties.AdaptiveQueryIndex U K),
          GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureSubmissionMeasurable (family query)) →
        ∀ (epsilon : ℝ),
          0 ≤ epsilon →
            ∀ (n : ℕ),
              0 < n →
                GHKR22BiasBounties.measureAdaptiveCheckerDeviationFailure law loss family epsilon n ≤
                  ↑(U * (U + 1) ^ K) * 2 * Real.exp (-↑n * epsilon ^ 2 / 32)
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\section*{Review row 8}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 717-\allowbreak{}726\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim source input}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{restatable}{theorem}{bountymonotone}
\label{thm:bounty-monotone-SC}
Fix any $\epsilon,\delta > 0$. Let $\cD \in \Delta(\cX \times \cY)$ be any distribution over labelled examples, and let $D \sim \cD^n$ be a holdout dataset consisting of $n$ i.i.d. samples from $\cD$. Suppose: \ifdraft
$$n \geq \frac{65 \ln\left(\frac{2(U+\frac{8}{\epsilon^3})}{\delta'}\right)}{\epsilon^3}.$$
\else $n \geq \frac{65 \ln\left(\frac{2(U+\frac{8}{\epsilon^3})}{\delta'}\right)}{\epsilon^3}.$\fi
Then for any (possibly adaptive) process generating a sequence of at most $U$ submissions $\{(g_i,h_i)\}_{i=1}^U$, with probability at least $1-\delta$, we have that MonotoneFalsifyAndUpdate$(\epsilon,D,\cdots)$ satisfies all of the properties proven in Theorem \ref{thm:bounty-SC} for FalsifyAndUpdate, and additionally satisfies the following error monotonicity property. Consider any model $f_t$ that is output, and any group $g_j \in \cG(f_t)$. Then:
\ifdraft
$$L(\cD,f_t,g_j) \leq \min_{\ell < t} L(\cD, f_{\ell}, g_j) + \frac{\epsilon}{\mu_\cD(g_j)} $$
\else $L(\cD,f_t,g_j) \leq \min_{\ell < t} L(\cD, f_{\ell}, g_j) + \frac{\epsilon}{\mu_\cD(g_j)}$. \fi
\end{restatable}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

For the shared-state restarting monotone process, the complete failure event has the exact sparse-transcript tail at query horizon U+floor(2/epsilon)^3, and outside it every published model satisfies the advertised historical-group monotonicity bound.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\section*{Review row 9}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 766-\allowbreak{}770\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim source input}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
The algorithmic problem we need to solve at each round $t$ is, given an existing model $f_{t-1}$, find a $(\mu,\Delta)$-certificate of optimality $(g,h) \in \cC$ for $f_{t-1}$ that maximizes $\mu\cdot \Delta$ as computed on the empirical data $D$ --- i.e. to solve:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:optproblem}
(g_t,h_t) =\arg\max_{(g,h) \in \cC }\mu_{D}(g)\cdot (L(D,f_{t-1},g) - L(D,h,g))
\end{equation}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}



\paragraph{Expanded PaperInterface specification}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}

  GHKR22BiasBounties.EmpiricalCandidateMaximizer loss sample current candidates chosen ↔
    chosen ∈ candidates ∧
      ∀ candidate ∈ candidates,
        GHKR22BiasBounties.empiricalCandidateObjective loss sample current candidate ≤
          GHKR22BiasBounties.empiricalCandidateObjective loss sample current chosen
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\section*{Review row 10}

\paragraph{Verbatim source input}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{lemma}[Adapted from \citep{gerrymandering}]
\label{lem:samplecomplexity}
Let $\cG$ denote a class of group indicator functions with VC-dimension $d_G$ and $\cH$ denote a class of binary models with VC-dimension $d_H$.  Let $f$ be an arbitrary binary model. Then if:
 $$n \geq \tilde O\left(\frac{(d_H + d_G) + \log(1/\delta)}{\eta^2} \right)$$
 we have that with probability $1-\delta$ over the draw of a dataset $D \sim \cD^n$, for every $g \in \cG$ and $h \in \cH$:
 $$\left|\mu_{\cD}(g)\cdot (L(\cD,f,g) - L(\cD,h,g)) - \mu_{D}(g_p)\cdot (L(D,f,g) - L(D,h,g)) \right| \leq \eta$$
\end{lemma}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

For every quantified g in G and h in H, the absolute difference between the population certificate objective for (g,h) and the empirical certificate objective for the same (g,h) is at most eta at the stated VC sample scale.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

$$n \geq \tilde O\left(\frac{(d_H + d_G) + \log(1/\delta)}{\epsilon^3} \right)$$

\end{restatable}

\paragraph{Verbatim source input}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{restatable}{theorem}{lemCSC}
\label{lem:CSC}
Fix an arbitrary distribution $\cD$ over $\cX\times \cY$, let $K$ be a class of ternary valued functions, and let $f:\cX\rightarrow \{0,1\}$ be any binary valued model. Let $p^*$ be a solution to the cost-sensitive classification problem $(\cD, K, \{c_{(x,y)}^f\})$, where $c_{(x,y)}^f$ are the induced costs of $f$. We have that:
\ifdraft
$$(g_{p^*},h_{p^*}) \in \arg\max_{(g,h) \in \cC_K }\mu_{\cD}(g)\cdot (L(\cD,f,g) - L(\cD,h,g))$$
\else $(g_{p^*},h_{p^*}) \in \arg\max_{(g,h) \in \cC_K }\mu_{\cD}(g)\cdot (L(\cD,f,g) - L(\cD,h,g))$. \fi
In other words, when $\cD$ is the empirical distribution over $D$, $(g_{p^*},h_{p^*})$ form a solution to optimization problem \eqref{eq:optproblem}.
\end{restatable}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

    ∀ (K : Set (GHKR22BiasBounties.TernaryPredictor X)) (pStar : GHKR22BiasBounties.TernaryPredictor X),
      (∀ p ∈ K, Measurable p) →
        GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureCostSensitiveMinimizer law current K pStar →
          GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureDerivedCertificateMaximizer law current K pStar
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{restatable}{lemma}{lemhopt}
\label{lem:hopt}
 Fix any $g_t \in \cG$ and dataset $D \in \cX^n$. Let $D_{g_t} = \{(x,y) \in \cD : g_t(x) = 1\}$ be the subset of $D$ consisting of members of group $g$. Let $h^* = \arg\min_{h \in \cH} L(D_{g_t},h)$. Then $h^*$ is a solution to optimization problem \ref{eq:hmin}.
\end{restatable}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{restatable}{lemma}{lemgopt}
\label{lem:gopt}
 Fix any $h_t \in \cH$ and dataset $D \in \cX^n$. Let:
 \ifdraft
 $$D_{h_t}^1 = \{(x,y) \in D : h_t(x) = y \neq f_{t-1}(x)\} \ \ \ \ D_{h_t}^0 = \{(x,y) \in D : h_t(x) \neq y = f_{t-1}(x)\}$$

$$D_{h_t} = \left(\bigcup_{(x,y) \in D_{h_t}^1} (x, 1)\right) \cup \left(\bigcup_{(x,y) \in D_{h_t}^0} (x, 0)\right)$$
\else $D_{h_t}^1 = \{(x,y) \in D : h_t(x) = y \neq f_{t-1}(x)\}$, $D_{h_t}^0 = \{(x,y) \in D : h_t(x) \neq y = f_{t-1}(x)\}$, and $D_{h_t} = \left(\bigcup_{(x,y) \in D_{h_t}^1} (x, 1)\right) \cup \left(\bigcup_{(x,y) \in D_{h_t}^0} (x, 0)\right)$. \fi
Let $g^* = \arg\min_{g \in \cG} L(D_{h_t},g)$. Then $g^*$ is a solution to optimization problem \eqref{eq:gmin}.
\end{restatable}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\section*{Review row 15}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 1008-\allowbreak{}1014\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim source input}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
\begin{restatable}{theorem}{localopt}
Let $D \in (\cX\times\cY)^n$ be an arbitrary dataset, $f_{t-1}:\cX\rightarrow \cY$ be an arbitrary model, $\cG$ and $\cH$ be arbitrary group and model classes, and $\epsilon > 0$. Then after solving at most $2/\epsilon$ empirical risk minimization problems over each of $\cG$ and $\cH$, AltMinCertificateFinder (Algorithm \ref{alg:altmin}) returns a $(\mu,\Delta)$-certificate of sub-optimality $(g_t,h_t)$ for $f_{t-1}$  that is an $\epsilon$-approximate local optimum (or saddle point) in the sense that:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item For every $h \in \cH$, $(g_t,h)$ is not a $(\mu', \Delta')$ certificate of sub-optimality for $f_{t-1}$ for any $\mu'\cdot \Delta' \geq \mu\cdot \Delta + \epsilon$, and
    \item For every $g \in \cG$, $(g,h_t)$ is not a $(\mu', \Delta')$ certificate of sub-optimality for $f_{t-1}$ for any $\mu'\cdot \Delta' \geq \mu\cdot \Delta + \epsilon$
\end{enumerate}
\end{restatable}
\end{ReviewVerbatim}


\paragraph{Formalized review target}
The archival source above is not asserted equivalent to this formalized target.
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
From an admissible initialization with positive certificate objective, repaired two-gap coordinate ascent returns a positive certificate that is epsilon-locally optimal in both coordinates; every strict-update trajectory has at most 2/epsilon updates.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\textbf{Archival source anchor:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 1008-\allowbreak{}1014\par}

                            some output ∧
                          GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureEpsilonCoordinatewiseLocal law current G H epsilon output ∧
                            GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureCertificateOfSuboptimality law
                              GHKR22BiasBounties.binaryZeroOneLoss current output.group output.replacement
                              (GHKR22BiasBounties.measureGroupMass law output.group)
                              (GHKR22BiasBounties.measureGroupLoss law GHKR22BiasBounties.binaryZeroOneLoss current
                                  output.group -
                                GHKR22BiasBounties.measureGroupLoss law GHKR22BiasBounties.binaryZeroOneLoss
                                  output.replacement output.group)) ∧
  (∀ {X : Type u_2} [inst : MeasurableSpace X] (law : MeasureTheory.Measure (X × Bool))
      [MeasureTheory.IsProbabilityMeasure law] {current : GHKR22BiasBounties.Model X Bool},
      GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasurableModel current →
        ∀ (epsilon : ℝ),
          0 < epsilon →
            ∀ (initial : GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificatePair X) (updates : List (GHKR22BiasBounties.CertificatePair X)),
              GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasureImprovingCoordinateSequence law current epsilon initial updates →
                GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasurableGroup initial.group →
                  GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasurableModel initial.replacement →
                    GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasurableGroup (updates.getLastD initial).group →
                      GHKR22BiasBounties.MeasurableModel (updates.getLastD initial).replacement →
                        ↑updates.length ≤ 2 / epsilon) ∧
    ∀ (epsilon : ℝ), 0 < epsilon → GHKR22BiasBounties.measureCoordinateAscentFuel epsilon + 1 = ⌈2 / epsilon⌉₊
\end{ReviewVerbatim}
